#7313. Revenue Accounting Manager at LiveKit

LiveKit is building the infrastructure layer for the voice-driven era of computing. Our platform gives developers everything they need to build, test, deploy, scale, and observe agents in production. Founded in 2021, LiveKit powers voice AI applications for OpenAI, xAI, Salesforce, Coursera, Spotify, and thousands of others, collectively facilitating billions of calls each year.

Our revenue is credit-denominated and consumption-based — usage billing, prepaid credit drawdown, and concurrency pricing across a growing enterprise base — which is close to the most complex revenue profile in infrastructure software. The load-bearing questions are open and there are few set policies and processes.

You will be the technical authority on revenue. You will own the entire Order to Cash cycle, from interfacing with our sales team, reviewing contracts, assessing terms in accordance with ASC 606, implementing workpapers and schedules, documenting conclusions, and assisting with billing and collection efforts. If building processes from square one and taking ownership of some of the most complex revenue arrangements excites you, this is the role for you.

You'll thrive as a Revenue Accounting Manager if you:

are obsessed with making the best product for our customers

are known as the go-to person for tackling tough problems

are focused on polish, detail and quality

are a fast learner, frequently picking up new tips, tricks, and skills

The best way to impress us is with thoughtful ways you'd implement LiveKit, and potentially tinkering with it 😊

Author our revenue accounting policy set — principal versus agent on brokered inference, breakage on expiring credits, performance obligation identification and allocation, minimum commitment versus overage, and contract modifications — and make each one stand up to an external auditor

Own the monthly revenue close: recognition, the deferred revenue rollforward, contract asset and liability balances, and the flux analysis

Own the revenue portion of our external audit, from the PBC list through the technical positions

Replace manual revenue entries with an automated billing-to-revenue reconciliation and a documented exception process — revenue should be computed by a system and reviewed by a person, with invoice accuracy above 99% and root-cause tracking on the misses

Govern order to cash: billing accuracy, dispute resolution, and an AR and collections process with real DSO and aging targets

Own commission capitalization and amortization under ASC 340-40

Review non-standard deal structures before they're signed, so the accounting consequence is a decision rather than a discovery

You're a licensed CPA with a Big 4 or comparable technical accounting foundation, plus real industry experience in a high-growth startup environment

You have demonstrable ASC 606 experience with usage-based or consumption revenue

You write technical memos that a reviewer can follow without a meeting: facts, guidance, analysis, conclusion

Experience assessing GAAP guidance and implementing it in real-world situations

You've owned a monthly revenue close hands-on, and you've closed revenue through at least one external audit

You're comfortable being the technical authority without a bench behind you

Billing system implementation or migration — Metronome, Stripe, Zuora

Enough SQL to validate a recognition schedule against raw usage data yourself

An opportunity to build something truly impactful to the world

Contribute to open source alongside world-class engineers

#7298. SDK Engineer - Python at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

We're looking for a SDK Engineer - Python to join our SDK team and own how developers talk to Supabase from Python. You'll work closely with the rest of the SDK team, our Auth, Realtime, Storage, PostgREST, Functions, CLI and Docs teams, and the open-source community, helping us make the Python SDK a library Python developers genuinely enjoy using.

Python developers reach Supabase from backend services, data pipelines, notebooks, and a growing number of AI agents and agent frameworks. The Python SDK is how all of them talk to the platform: database queries, auth, storage, realtime and edge functions, in both sync and async flavors.

This is a library-authoring role, and the work happens in the open: public repos, public issue trackers, and API decisions whose consequences last for years. If you get satisfaction from a type checker that catches the bug before your users do, from an async client that cleans up after itself, and from an issue tracker that isn't a graveyard, you'll like it here.

This role is ideal for someone who thrives in async, fast-paced environments and is excited about building developer tools that scale to millions.

Build and evolve our Python SDK across database, auth, storage, realtime and functions, with both sync and async APIs that feel natural in Python.

Start new things. Alongside the maintenance work, find what's missing, make the case for it, and build it. Nobody will hand you the list.

Work in the open. Triage inbound issues, review and shepherd outside contributions, keep CI and release automation healthy, and hold a clear, kind line on scope.

Own the typing experience. Make the SDK fully typed and checker-friendly, so autocomplete and static analysis stay correct in real codebases.

Keep us honest across environments. Multiple Python versions, sync and async frameworks, workers, serverless and notebooks — with dependency ranges that don't fight our users' projects. Test it, don't assume it.

Work on realtime. Long-lived websocket connections, reconnection, and subscription and presence semantics.

Track the platform. When auth, realtime, storage, postgrest or edge functions ship a change, make sure the SDK reflects it, and that we say so in the changelog.

Design in public. Write the RFCs, migration guides, release notes and docs. Treat a breaking change as a deprecation path plus a migration story, not a version bump.

Make our SDK legible to AI coding agents, which are now a major consumer of it.

Know modern Python typing deeply. You use generics, protocols, overloads and TypedDict as design tools, run strict type checking, and fix type errors without reaching for Any.

Have authored or maintained a published library with a public API and users outside your own company. You know what it costs to change something people depend on.

Know the maintainer side of open source. Issue triage at volume, reviewing outside PRs, release automation, changelogs and semver, security reports, and saying no to a feature request without losing the person who asked.

Are fluent in asyncio and have opinions on how a library should serve both sync and async callers.

Know Python packaging for libraries: pyproject.toml, wheels, modern tooling, version support policy, and why libraries shouldn't pin their dependencies tightly.

Test as a habit: unit, type-level and end-to-end against a real stack. Every fix comes with a regression test.

Write well. Design docs, changelogs and issue replies are a large part of the output here, much of it public, and we're async by default.

Have real autonomy: you find the important work, scope it, ship it, and say what you didn't do.

Communicate clearly across both technical and non-technical audiences

Have experience in async or globally distributed teams

Are energized by solving real-world problems for developers

Are comfortable navigating ambiguity and moving quickly

Bonus points if you: have run a major-version migration for a public package, know your way around JWT/OAuth, have built integrations for AI agent frameworks, or have generated idiomatic clients from a schema.

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.

#7289. Engineering Manager, Automation Intelligence at Fingerprint

Fingerprint empowers enterprises to detect and stop online fraud with the world’s most accurate device intelligence.  We lead our industry with bleeding-edge identification capabilities and work on turning new ideas and discoveries in the fraud detection space into reality. Our customers range from innovative startups to leading enterprise companies, including Plaid, Dropbox, and Booking.com.

Fingerprint is a globally dispersed, 100% remote company. We were named on on the 2026 Forbes Best Startup Employers list and ranked #803 on the 2026 Inc. 5000 list of America’s fastest-growing private companies.

We have raised $77M and are backed by Craft Ventures (Tesla, Facebook, Airbnb ), Nexus Venture Partners ( Postman, Apollo.io, MinIO, Druva) and Uncorrelated Ventures ( Redis, Rollbar,  Gradle).

Fingerprint's device intelligence platform processes billions of identification events for companies that need to know who — or what — is on the other end of a request. Increasingly, the answer is not a person. AI agents, AI assistants, crawlers, headless browsers, and malicious automation now make up a large and fast-growing share of internet traffic, and telling them apart from humans — and from each other — is becoming one of the most important problems our customers ask us to solve.

We are standing up a dedicated Automation Intelligence engineering team to own that problem end to end: detection in the browser, detection at the edge, the classification taxonomy that tells customers what kind of automation they're seeing and how confident we are, and the API that customers and their agents call.

We're looking for an Engineering Manager to lead this team. You'll inherit a small, senior group (three engineers to start, growing to four or five) and a clear mandate. You report directly to the VP of Engineering.

This is a hands-on-adjacent engineering manager role for a small, senior, high-leverage team. You are not expected to write the production code — your engineers and their agents do that — but you are expected to be close enough to it to set direction, evaluate the work, and hold the quality bar.

The team has unusual autonomy and unusual visibility. It owns a product that leadership considers strategically critical, it is the first team operating Fingerprint's AI-native engineering model, and its results — good and bad — will be used as evidence for how the rest of engineering works. That means two things: you'll have real room to decide how the team operates, and you'll need to be honest about what the data shows.

In the first 90 days you will likely: finalize the roster and ownership lines with the sibling team, get the quality loop and engineering baselines running, deliver the engineering evidence for the Beta gate, and run the first review of the AI-native operating model.

Our customers range from innovative startups to leading enterprise companies, including Plaid, Dropbox, and Booking.com.

Fingerprint is a globally dispersed, 100% remote company. We were named on on the 2026 Forbes Best Startup Employers list and ranked #803 on the 2026 Inc. 5000 list of America’s fastest-growing private companies.

Fingerprint's device intelligence platform processes billions of identification events for some of the world's most security-conscious companies — financial institutions, fraud prevention operators, and enterprises that rely on us to stop attackers. That makes our own application security a product feature, not a back-office function: our customers' adversaries are, by definition, motivated to probe what we build.

As we scale with large enterprise customers, we're investing in application security as a dedicated, first-class engineering discipline. Security is already part of how our teams build — scanning in the pipeline, a Cloud Platform team that treats security as a partner, and a mature compliance posture — and we're now standing up a focused AppSec function, led by a Senior Manager, Application Security who owns the program and strategy, to take it to the next level. You will be the founding senior engineer of that function — the person who turns strategy into a working secure SDLC, finds and fixes what matters, and raises the security bar across engineering through technical depth, tooling, and influence.

This is a hands-on Staff role. You will design, build, and operate — not just advise.

Threat Modeling, Security Reviews & Vulnerability Management

Incident Response & Detection (application layer)

This is the senior individual-contributor counterpart to the Senior Manager, Application Security. The manager owns the program, the strategy, and the team; you own the technical depth and the execution. The two roles are designed to work as a pair: you'll bring the findings, the engineering judgment, and the ground truth from inside the codebase, and together you'll decide what to prioritize.

As a Staff engineer, you are expected to operate with a high degree of independence — scoping ambiguous problems, setting technical direction for AppSec, and driving multi-quarter initiatives without needing day-to-day direction. As the founding engineer of a dedicated AppSec function, the patterns, tooling, and standards you establish will be the foundation the practice grows on.

This is not a role for someone who wants to primarily write policy or manage a program from a distance. It's for an engineer who finds real bugs, builds real tooling, and earns the trust of other engineers by making their code better.

At Fingerprint, we believe in pay transparency — it's part of how we operate as a distributed, remote-first team.  Salaries are based on function, level, and geographic location, benchmarked against similar stage growth companies.

We are currently hiring a Staff Security Engineer within the Imunify360 product.

Imunify360 is CloudLinux's security suite for Linux web servers. It protects hundreds of thousands of hosting servers and the websites running on them, so it sees most large-scale web attacks early — often in the first hours.

Check out our website for more information: https://imunify360.com/

At Imunify360, protecting customers from evolving cyber threats requires close collaboration between security research, engineering, and other teams. As a Staff Security Engineer, you'll take end-to-end technical ownership of complex security challenges, from initial threat discovery and analysis to detection, mitigation, and post-incident review.

As an individual contributor, you won't have direct reports or people management responsibilities. All security research and testing are conducted within authorized boundaries, with clearly defined scope and responsible disclosure practices.

Your mission is to strengthen Imunify360's ability to detect, prevent, and respond to emerging threats, delivering measurable security improvements that protect customers at scale.

#7274. Staff Engineer, Imunify360 (remote work) at CloudLinux

CloudLinux is a global remote-first company. We are driven by our principles: do the right thing, employees first, we are remote first, and we deliver high-volume, low-cost Linux infrastructure and security products that help companies to increase the efficiency of their operations. Every person on our team supports each other and does what we can to ensure we all are successful.

Check out our website for more information: cloudlinux.com

We are currently hiring Staff Engineers for various projects within the Imunify360 product.

Imunify360 is CloudLinux's multi-layer security suite for Linux web servers: antivirus and malware cleanup, WAF, IDS/IPS, Proactive Defense, patch management (KernelCare/Patchman), and IP/domain reputation. It runs on hundreds of thousands of shared, VPS and dedicated hosting servers, under cPanel, Plesk, DirectAdmin and standalone.

The engineering org is 60+ engineers in squads covering backend, core, server agent, malware research, WAF/WebShield, and Patchman. Core services are Python; agents and performance-critical paths are Go and Rust. We run on Hetzner and our own bare metal.

Check out our website for more information: https://imunify360.com/

Most complex technical challenges in Imunify360 go beyond individual squads. A single detection change can impact the scanner, agent, UI, reputation backend, and release process. While squad leads own delivery within their teams, the Staff Engineer ensures technical alignment across the product.

As a Staff Engineer, you'll be a hands-on technical leader working across the entire product, focusing on the most critical and high-impact challenges as priorities evolve. You'll drive technical decisions, improve architecture, establish engineering standards, and help teams build sustainable solutions.

This is an individual contributor role, without direct people management responsibilities. You'll remain actively involved in development, spending approximately 30–50% of your time writing production code, rather than focusing solely on architectural design.

While deep expertise in a particular area is valued, this role requires the flexibility to work across different components, collaborate with multiple squads, and tackle challenges wherever your expertise can make the greatest impact.

#7273. Carrot: Telemedicine Registered Nurse (RN), Consultant

Headquarters: Remote

About Carrot:

Carrot is the leading global fertility and family care platform, built on intelligent care orchestration: the right clinical guidance, at the right moment, in the context of each member’s life. More than a thousand multinational employers, health plans, and health systems trust Carrot to support millions of members across 195 countries – from pre-pregnancy through menopause and major life moments in between. Carrot's comprehensive clinical program delivers industry-leading cost savings for plan sponsors and award-winning experiences and improved outcomes for millions of people worldwide.

Carrot is widely regarded as a defining force in healthcare innovation as a recipient of several top-tier awards, including Fast Company's 'Most Innovative Companies' and CNBC's '100 Barrier Breaking Startups'. The company is regularly cited by leading global outlets — including The Economist, Bloomberg, The Wall Street Journal, NPR, ABC News, and Harvard Business Review — as a leading voice on digital health, the future of work, and family health. Learn more at get-carrot.com.

The Opportunity

Carrot is expanding its telemedicine clinic with a network of part-time to full-time, fully remote Registered Nurses who deliver evidence-based virtual care to members across all U.S. states. As an RN in this network, you are often the first clinical touchpoint for members navigating fertility, menopause, hormonal, and metabolic health journeys, providing triage, education, care coordination, and documentation that shape the entire care experience.

All work in this role is performed within RN scope of practice. RNs provide triage, education, care coordination, and documentation; they do not diagnose, do not prescribe, and do not independently modify a treatment plan. Anything requiring clinical judgment beyond RN scope is routed to a physician, so sound escalation judgment is central to the role.

This is a contractor engagement of approximately 20-40 hours per week, with scheduled shifts within the Monday-Friday, 8:00a-6:00p CST coverage window. 

What You’ll Own

In this role, you will:

  • Serve as the first clinical reviewer of inbound member messages, triaging for urgency, escalating to a physician when needed, and routing non-clinical threads to the appropriate support team.
  • Deliver member education on menopause staging, hormone therapy, preconception health, metabolic health, and medication administration.
  • Review and triage incoming lab results, escalate critical findings per protocol, retrieve outside records, and coordinate specialist referrals.
  • Manage pharmacy callbacks and prior authorizations in coordination with the care team.
  • Document all clinically relevant interactions same-day, create tracked internal tasks with named owners, and follow established escalation pathways.
  • Maintain active NLC multi-state licensure, complete required credentialing and onboarding, and adhere to HIPAA, continuing education, and liability coverage requirements.
  • Contribute to chart audits and clinical protocol feedback as the telemedicine program scales.

What Success Looks Like  

In your first 12–18 months, you will have:

  • Established a consistent record of accurate, protocol-aligned triage with appropriate physician escalation and no missed critical lab values.
  • Maintained same-day documentation and timely lab follow-up, with no outstanding credentialing or compliance gaps.
  • Delivered high-quality member education across menopause, preconception, hormone therapy, and metabolic health topics, reflected in strong member experience results.
  • Managed care coordination workflows reliably within your coverage window, including referrals, records retrieval, prior authorizations, and pharmacy callbacks.
  • Contributed to chart audits, protocol feedback, and quality improvement as the telemedicine program scales.

About You

You likely have:

  • An active, unrestricted RN license with NLC (Nurse Licensure Compact) multi-state privileges, in good standing, plus an ADN or BSN from an accredited program (BSN preferred).
  • A minimum of 3 years of active RN clinical experience, with meaningful experience in women's health, reproductive health, OB/GYN, fertility, endocrinology, Urology, Mens Health, or menopause care.
  • Demonstrated nurse triage experience, including assessing urgency, applying protocol, escalating appropriately, and recognizing critical lab values.
  • A clear understanding of RN scope of practice and sound judgment about when to escalate.
  • Strong written communication for asynchronous messaging and a strong phone presence for symptom assessment and instruction.
  • Proficiency with EHR and telehealth systems, secure messaging, and e-fax workflows.
  • Familiarity with HIPAA and applicable telemedicine privacy, security, and regulatory requirements.
  • The ability to commit to scheduled shifts within the Monday-Friday, 8:00a-6:00p CST coverage window, with a HIPAA-compliant, private home workspace and reliable internet.

Nice to Have ⭐

While not required, we would be especially excited if you bring:

  • Experience in a dedicated menopause clinic, fertility center, REI practice, or comparable specialty program.
  • Familiarity with preconception and fertility workup, including AMH and hormone panels, ovulation tracking, and cycle-monitoring labs.
  • Hormone therapy education experience, including prior authorization workflows.
  • Experience supporting members on anti-obesity medications, including GLP-1 receptor agonists.
  • Prior experience in digital health, health benefits, or multi-state telemedicine environments.
  • Experience contributing to clinical protocol development, quality improvement, or member-facing clinical content.
  • Experience supporting diverse and historically underserved populations, including LGBTQ+ and gender-diverse individuals.
  • Relevant certification (e.g., MSCP through The Menopause Society, RNC-OB) and/or fluency in an additional language.

 

Why This Role Matters

Carrot's RN network is the front line of the telemedicine clinic. RNs are typically the first clinician a member reaches, and the quality of their triage judgment, education, and follow-through directly determines member safety, trust, and the experience of care during some of the most personal moments of members' lives.

This role also keeps the clinical program running well as it scales. Reliable triage, same-day documentation, and disciplined escalation protect physician capacity for the decisions that require it, while expanding access to guideline-aligned fertility, menopause, and metabolic health support for members who often cannot find it locally.

Why Candidates Join Carrot

Carrot offers the opportunity to do meaningful work in service of a deeply human mission.

Highlights include:

  • A mission-driven company focused on expanding access to fertility and family-forming care globally.
  • A high-growth environment where leaders are empowered to shape strategy and drive impact.
  • Competitive compensation, equity, and comprehensive benefits.
  • Flexible Time Off and a culture that actively encourages employees to recharge.
  • Comprehensive parental leave and family-forming benefits.
  • A collaborative, inclusive, and values-driven culture.
  • The opportunity to work alongside passionate, thoughtful teammates committed to improving healthcare outcomes worldwide.

Compensation: 

Carrot offers a holistic Total Rewards package designed to support our employees in all aspects of their life inside and outside of work, including health and wellness benefits, retirement savings plans, short- and long-term incentives, parental leave, family-forming assistance, and a competitive compensation package. The starting base salary for this position will range from $40-$50/ hr. Actual compensation may vary from posted base salary depending on your confirmed job-related skills and experience. 

 

Fraud and Security Notice: Please note that all communication regarding job opportunities at Carrot will come exclusively from an @get-carrot.com email address. If you receive messages from any other domain, please disregard them and report the incident to: securityreporting@get-carrot.com

 

A Note on AI

At Carrot, we believe AI can help us work smarter, move faster, and create more meaningful experiences for our members, customers, and each other. While not every role requires deep technical AI expertise, we value people who are curious, eager to learn, and excited to thoughtfully incorporate AI into their day-to-day work.

For some roles, AI fluency is a core requirement—and we'll call that out clearly in the qualifications. For everyone else, we're looking for people who embrace new ways of working, use AI responsibly to improve quality and efficiency, and continue to learn as the technology evolves.

Why Carrot?

Carrot has received national and international recognition for its pioneering work, including Fast Company's Most Innovative Companies and World Changing Ideas, Inc. Power Partners, and Modern Healthcare’s Innovators. Carrot’s global workforce has been acknowledged with several accolades, including Fortune’s Best Workplaces in Healthcare, Great Place to Work, and Age-Friendly Employer certifications. Carrot is regularly featured in media reporting on issues related to the future of work, women in leadership, and healthcare innovation, including MSNBC, The Economist, Bloomberg, The Wall Street Journal, CNBC, National Public Radio, Harvard Business Review, and more. Learn more at carrotfertility.com.

#7270. Here Are the Cable News Ratings for October 2026

MS NOW and CNN reversed fortunes as both networks finished October on an upward swing in total viewers and the Adults 25-54 demo during primetime and total day. This uplift comes after two back-to-back months of viewership losses.

MS NOW also made recent history, topping CNN in the demo during weekend live programming (7 a.m.-6 p.m. ET) for the first time in 14 years, since November 2012. MS NOW had 50,000 vs. CNN’s 48,000 demo viewers.

The network also surpassed CNN in the total day demo, moving ahead of the Skydance-owned channel courtesy of 2,000 additional demo viewers.

Meanwhile, Fox News had its third consecutive month of viewership losses in both measured categories during both dayparts.

According to Nielsen Big Data + Panel, Fox News had 1.675 million total viewers in primetime and 145,000 viewers in the adults 25-54 demo in October. During total day, it had 1.101 million total viewers and 98,000 viewers in the demo.

Compared to its September 2026 performance, Fox News was down 2% in total viewers and 7% in the A25-54 demo during primetime. In total day, the network fell 1% in total viewers and 8% in the demo compared with the previous month.

Looking at Fox News’ performance against the same month in 2025, it declined by 28% in total viewers and 36% in the demo during primetime. While in total day, it was down by 27% in total viewers and 32% in the demo—the only cable network with year-over-year declines in both measured categories.

The network finished October in second place among total viewers and was fourth in the demo during primetime. During total day, it remained in first place in total viewers and was No. 3 in the demo.

MS NOW finished second among news networks in total viewers in primetime and total day, with 925,000 and 588,000 viewers, respectively, in October. In the A25-54 demo, the network had 95,000 viewers in primetime and 62,000 during total day.

Compared to September, MS NOW was up 10% in total viewers and 20% in the A25-54 demo during primetime. During total day, it also had increases of 8% in total viewers and 19% in the demo.

Looking at its performance in October 2025, MS NOW was up 13% in total viewers and 30% in the demo during primetime. It was also up 17% in total viewers and 38% in the demo during total day.

MS NOW was the No. 5 cable network during primetime and No. 3 during total day among total viewers. It finished 14th in the demo during primetime and jumped from 11th to eighth during total day.

In October primetime, CNN had 598,000 total viewers and 107,000 A25-54 viewers. Looking at total day, the network had 402,000 total viewers and 60,000 demo viewers.

Compared to the previous month, CNN grew by 11% in total viewers and 19% in demo viewers during primetime. It was up by 8% in total viewers and 13% in the demo during total day.

When looking at its performance to the same month in 2025 during primetime, CNN had gains of 19% in total viewers and 39% in the demo. It was also up 4% during total day and 3% in the demo.

CNN finished sixth in primetime and fifth in total day in total viewers among all cable networks. In the demo, it finished up two spots to ninth in primetime and was also ninth in total day.

#7264. Brandweek 2026: Can Too Much Attribution Make Marketing Worse? ft. Amanda Teder

Attribution’s a tricky thing. It can help marketers make better decisions while simultaneously making their strategy worse.

Live from the Marketing Vanguard stage at Brandweek 2026, Jenny Rooney sits down with Amanda Teder, CMO of Generac, to unpack what happens when marketing gets too focused on what is easiest to measure.

Amanda breaks down the full-funnel operating model she has built at Generac, and why demand generation is only the opening move in a high-consideration category.

She also discusses why some purchase journeys need nurturing for months or even years.

This episode is part of a special vidcast series that was recorded live during Brandweek 2026 and presented in partnership with Tatari.

Amanda Teder is Chief Marketing Officer at Generac, an energy solutions company serving both residential and industrial customers.

Before joining Generac, Amanda spent roughly 20 years at Procter & Gamble, where she worked across brands including Oral-B and Febreze, before moving to Michelin.

Across those very different categories, one theme has stayed consistent: understand what actually drives the business, then build the systems that can turn customer insight into repeatable growth. And, at Generac, that has meant connecting demand generation, nurture, sales enablement and post-purchase support into a more complete customer journey.

[07:41] Lead Generation Isn’t Your Finish Line — Amanda argues that demand generation is not enough for a considered purchase. Generac’s model follows the customer through awareness, nurture, sales support and post-purchase experience. The aim is to build a system that can turn that interest into revenue across the entire journey.

[10:05] Great CMOs Know What Kind of Marketer They Are — Amanda describes herself as a builder. She likes entering a business, identifying the critical growth levers and putting the systems in place to make those levers work repeatedly. That self-awareness matters more to her than chasing a brand because it happens to look exciting from the outside; the opportunity is in what you can build.

[13:10] The AI Debate Is Starting With the Wrong Question — At Brandweek 2026, Amanda pushes past the familiar “AI versus humans” framing. Before deciding what AI should do and what people should keep, marketers need to ask why the work exists in the first place. Strategy, prioritization, judgment and taste have to come first, otherwise, automation simply helps you do the wrong thing faster.

[14:08] Attribution Can Make Marketing Worse — Amanda’s unpopular marketing opinion is one worth sitting with: too much focus on attribution can drag investment toward the bottom of the funnel because that is where outcomes are easiest to measure. The risk is that marketers sacrifice the brand and demand-building work that creates future opportunity. Measurement should sharpen strategy, not shrink it.

Jenny Rooney is Chief Brand and Community Officer, leading strategy for the overall ADWEEK brand as well as the ways in which we serve and support our audiences with high-value content, products, partnerships and experiences, notably through our community programs such as Marketing Vanguard.

Select ADWEEK as one of your favorite sources and stay up to date on our latest content.

Plus, why doing the right thing beats the short-term number.

You've built observability good enough for AI agents to handle some on-call load. You've documented your decisions so others can build on your work.

Running production infrastructure on AWS at scale, including managed relational databases, networking, and load balancing

#7253. Anthropic Strikes Its First Licensing Deal, With the AP, for Election Coverage

This story was originally published in On Background with Mark Stenberg, a free, weekly newsletter that explores the key themes shaping the media industry. You can sign up for it here.

If, by chance, you opened Claude on Election Night and asked it for an update on the latest polling data, you would have been treated to a novel sight: up-the-minute information, provided by and attributed to the Associated Press.

This new development was the result of a content and data licensing deal struck between the two organizations, which was struck just before the midterm elections, according to two people familiar with the matter.

Anthropic did not respond to a request for comment, and the AP did not provide a comment on the record. The financial specifics of the arrangement, as well as other critical details, such as its timeframe, were not disclosed by either party.

Nonetheless, the deal marks a dramatic evolution in strategy for Anthropic, as the artificial intelligence firm behind Claude had not, until Tuesday, struck a content licensing deal with any digital publisher.

In that regard, Claude differed dramatically from its primary competitor, ChatGPT, whose parent company OpenAI has negotiated more than a dozen content licensing deals with major publishers, including Axel Springer, News Corp., and The Atlantic, among others.

In fact, the reluctance by Anthropic to engage in any kind of content licensing discussion had become such a subject of intrigue within the industry that I explored the situation in August, in one of my more literally titled pieces.

At the time, digital media executives told me that the company genuinely believes that its scraping and use of publisher content is legally permissible under the concept of fair use. As such, it had not moved to enter into any such partnerships, nor had it indicated any plans to do so in the future.

So why change its tactics now, especially as the company prepares to go public in a matter of weeks?

FOR Scoops, Sharp Analysis, and Insider Context

The likely answer is that Anthropic finally admitted what publishers have long known to be true, which is that an information index without access to up-to-date data is dramatically limited in its utility.

Because Anthropic refused to strike content licensing deals with major news publishers, it lacked access to the kind of credible, timely reporting that consumers turn to in key moments like an election cycle.

Without the ability to intake data from premium publishers, whose websites block its crawlers, Anthropic could only cite what AI researcher Deana Burke calls the “unlocked web.” These low-credibility sites might serve as sufficient sources of information for low-stakes queries, but they cannot tell a user which politician won a Senate seat 10 minutes ago.

As such, Anthropic was facing down the prospect of its users finding its platform to be nearly useless as a source of real-time information on one of the most important days of the year for search queries.

Publishers had been blocking Anthropic, as well as other answer engines, to produce this very outcome. The tactic is effectively a digital embargo, with news outlets withholding their content in hopes of bringing answer engines to the bargaining table to strike licensing deals. And in this limited instance, the strategy worked.

Of course, the AP is a bit of an exceptional case: It generates the vast majority of its revenue via licensing and wire deals with other publishers, so much of its business is structured around striking these kinds of tie-ups already. The AP was also the first publisher with whom Google and OpenAI signed licensing agreements.

The AP also has the advantage of being a canonically non-partisan outlet—it positions itself as more of a service, like Getty Images for information, than a standard publisher, which often has an editorial slant one way or the other. For these reasons, the AP makes for an ideal partner for an AI firm looking to test out licensing opportunities.

This limited foray into licensing is by no means an indicator that Anthropic has irrevocably changed its licensing strategy. In all likelihood, the company will use the data from this experiment to determine whether it wants to go further down this path or not.

But the deal does make a compelling case for the value of credible, real-time information to answer engines. And if Anthropic wants to stake its claim as the dominant platform in the ecosystem, it is hard to imagine how it does so without premium publishers.

Closing on OpenWeb (SCOOP): The venture firm Savage Ventures is preparing a bid of between $60 million to $70 million to acquire OpenWeb, the bankrupt Israeli adtech firm once worth $1.5 billion, according to an inside source. A distressed sale would be the latest grim chapter in the long, sordid saga of OpenWeb, whose fortunes have fallen precipitously in recent years. The startup, whose 2021 Series E received investment from The New York Times, helps publishers monetize their comments sections, a commercial model whose appeal is dwindling as the open web continues to contract. Still, its data, publisher relationships, and on-page real estate make it a reasonable acquisition target at just 4% of its high watermark valuation.

Creator Agency Consolidation (EXCLUSIVE): On Thursday, the social-first agency LV8 merged with its sister company Mirage Digital. Following the merger, the newly combined firm raised a round of equity funding “in the high eight figures” from Velocity Capital, according to LV8 chief executive Griffin Haddrill. The deal is the latest in a series of creator and social-centric tie-ups in the agency space in recent weeks. In September alone, the social agency SAMY acquired the influencer shop Get Engaged, and Billion Dollar Boy bought the business-to-business influencer marketing agency Creator Authority. In the first half of 2026, there were 70 creator economy M&A transactions, up 23% year-over-year, according to Quartermast Advisors, and agencies have accounted for around a fifth of those. Expect more from me on this front in the next week or two!

People Inc. People: On Thursday, People Inc. laid off around 6% of its staff, a total of nearly 200 employees, according to an internal memo I obtained. The cuts primarily affected its print and “legacy digital” businesses, the latest in a growing constellation of data points that reflect the diminishing commercial prospects of the open web. Of course, People Inc. is not alone in its reductions: Netflix, Disney, and Skydance, among others, have all enacted layoffs ahead of the holiday season.

On Thursday, at the Creator Marketing Summit in New York, I spoke on-stage with Tyler Stewart, the head of marketing and creative partnerships at Gopuff. Before the panel, I asked Tyler about the news that had broken earlier that morning, that two of his competitors—Instacart and Doordash—planned to combine in a $17 billion merger.

He offered, understandably, a rehearsed response: that Gopuff is in a category of its own because it owns its inventory and delivers from its own locations, unlike the middleman services its peers provide. That point of differentiation is a cornerstone of the Gopuff business; but another, more salient difference is its heavy use of creator marketing and collaboration.

In June, Gopuff launched a coconut water brand with Tom Brady, and last month Druski took an equity stake in the company as part of a partnership. The tactics stem largely from fiscal responsibility—Tyler said several times that budgetary constraints were a factor in his decision-making—but it also positions the company to capitalize on the growing momentum behind the creator space.

Mark Stenberg: Creators are a cornerstone of Gopuff’s marketing. How did that come about, and how has the strategy evolved?

Tyler Stewart: We’re still a privately funded startup, and we do not have the budget, frankly, to spend crazy TV dollars. Back in the day, our approach was to scale, spray, and pray, but we’ve evolved. Now, we want to go deeper and longer with a core crew of partners who can help us punch above our weight and reach audiences we can’t reach ourselves.

Mark: You also build products with creators. How does that work?

Tyler: Because we’re first party, we have a full private-label business and manufacturing capabilities that we can bring to our partners, which allows us to have a little fun with it. But it’s also more complex. A lot of times, creators don’t know what they want to do or how to do it, so we need to be that partner for them.

Mark: What’s the best example of what this model can unlock?

Tyler: Two years ago for Halloween, we made a massive chocolate bar with Alix Earle. We delivered it to her house in L.A. for her party and generated content around it. Then we made a regular-size version for our whole network, and it was gone in three minutes.

Mark: How do you decide which creators to work with?

Tyler: There are massive celebrities we’ve onboarded thinking, “They have hundreds of millions of followers, they’re going to sell the shit out of this.” And then they don’t. So, can they get us eyeballs we wouldn’t get otherwise? Do they have a level of authenticity, where they actually give a shit about the product? What’s proven not to work for us is the transactional version.

Mark: Druski’s deal with Gopuff included equity. How do you think about ownership for creators?

Tyler: We don’t have cash, so we need somebody who believes in us and wants to ride this wave with us. Druski is hilarious, and we’re going to let him do what he does best. But he has to accept that he’s not getting the check that PrizePicks or T-Mobile would pay him for a commercial.

Mark: You can’t give equity to every creator. What does it take to earn it?

Tyler: Can you help us grow our brand and business? Not everybody can. We put a lot of performance deals in place: Help us get a certain number of customers, and I’ll give you more equity. Why would the business want you as an owner if you’re not helping the business?

#7251. Growth Marketer, Creators & Channels at Supabase

Supabase is the Postgres development platform, built by developers for developers. We provide a complete backend solution including Database, Auth, Storage, Edge Functions, Realtime, and Vector Search. All services are deeply integrated and designed for growth.

Creator and UGC content is only getting bigger as a growth channel for developer tools, and right now Supabase reaches it mostly at arm's length, through agencies that own the actual relationships with creators. This role exists to change that: bring our creator/influencer program fully in-house, own those relationships directly, and grow the program into a multi-million-dollar channel. A good marker of success looks like a roster of around 50 active creators within the first year — at which point we take what worked and go find the next channel to unlock the same way.

This is a growth and marketing role first, not a DevRel or engineering-adjacent one — we're optimizing less for technical depth and more for someone outgoing enough that creators want to work with them directly, creative enough to keep testing new formats and strategies, and operationally strong enough to run the budgets and reporting behind it. The work is currently split on an interim basis across a couple of people on the marketing team; this role is being created to own it end to end.

Source, recruit, and manage direct relationships with content creators (TikTok, Instagram, YouTube)

Design and run creator campaigns end to end

Find and test new acquisition channels and strategies — lots of small experiments, doubling down on what works

Operationalize and report on these programs: budgets, tracking, results

Grow with the role: the remit could develop into owning all content from a growth perspective, beyond creators and UGC

3+ years in growth, partnerships or creator marketing at a dev tool, SaaS or consumer tech company

Track record of launching channels from zero — and turning winners into repeatable programs

Outgoing relationship-builder creators genuinely want to work with

Strong operator. Comfortable with spreadsheets.

Ideally already doing a version of this somewhere scrappier or more startup — someone thriving at a smaller company is a better signal here than someone comfortable (but not standout) at a big-name platform

We hire globally. We believe you can do your best work from anywhere. There are no Supabase offices, but we provide a WeWork membership or co-working allowance you can use anywhere in the world.

Every team member receives ESOP (equity ownership) in the company. We want everyone to share in the upside of what we’re building together.

Use this budget to set up your ideal work environment—laptop, monitor, headphones, or whatever helps you do your best work.

Supabase covers 100% of health insurance for employees and 80% for dependents, wherever you are. Your wellbeing and your family’s health are important to us.

Once a year, the entire company gathers in a new city for a week of connection, collaboration, and fun. It’s a highlight of our year.

We operate asynchronously and trust you to manage your own time. You know what needs to be done and when.

Every team member receives an annual education allowance to spend on learning—courses, books, conferences, or anything that supports your growth.

Supabase was born-remote and open-source-first. We believe our globally distributed team is our secret weapon in building tools developers love.

Over $1B raised (including our $500M Series F)

We move fast, build in public, and use what we ship. If it’s in your project, we probably use it in ours too. We believe deeply in the open-source ecosystem and strive to support—not replace—existing tools and communities.

For anyone who genuinely cares about voice AI, not as a trend to chase, but as a technology to build, this is the place where the most interesting problems at the most interesting scale are being solved by a team small enough that you'll actually know everyone's name.

AssemblyAI builds the foundational speech models behind the Voice AI products our customers build. The people who train those models and build the systems that serve them are some of the hardest people in tech to hire; and we need to hire a lot more of them in 2027.

We’re looking for a Senior Technical Recruiter to own hiring for our Research team and a portfolio of senior Engineering roles: Researchers, Research Engineers, and ML Engineers. You’ll work directly with our research and engineering leaders, and you’ll be expected to understand the work well enough to find, assess, and close people others can’t.

AssemblyAI strives to recruit and retain exceptional talent from diverse backgrounds while ensuring pay equity across our team. Our salary ranges are set to be competitive for our size, stage, and industry, and reflect just one component of the full compensation, benefits, and rewards we offer.

Salary determinations consider a variety of factors, including relevant experience, technical depth, skills demonstrated during the interview process, and maintaining internal equity with peers on the team. The range shared below represents a general expectation for the posted position. However, we are open to considering candidates who may fall above or below the outlined experience level—in those cases, we will communicate any adjustments to the expected salary range.

The range provided applies to candidates located in the United States. For candidates outside of the U.S., compensation ranges may differ; any adjustments will be communicated throughout the interview process.

The expected cash compensation for this role is listed above. Our total compensation package includes competitive equity grants and 100% employer-paid benefits.

#7213. GRC Analyst at Supabase

We’re looking for a GRC Analyst to join our Compliance & Security team and help make risk management, audit and compliance a continuous part of how Supabase runs. You’ll work closely with engineering, security, IT, and legal to help us define our risk tolerance, keep our compliance programs (including SOC 2 and ISO/IEC 27001:2022) current, test controls on a rolling basis, and answer customer and vendor security reviews quickly and accurately.

This role is ideal for someone who thrives in async, fast-paced environments and is excited about building developer tools that scale to millions.

Run risk assessments across the company and major product changes. Score likelihood and impact, propose treatments, and bring the results to leadership to inform risk tolerance decisions.

Maintain the risk register, track remediation to closure, and report status to compliance and security leadership.

Monitor controls on a rolling basis, using automated tests where they exist and manual testing where they don’t. Triage failures, prioritize by the risk register, and align each finding with an owner and a due date.

Support internal audit, whether performed by an external partner or in-house: propose scope from the risk register, supply evidence, and track findings to closure.

Run day-to-day operations for our compliance programs: evidence collection, control owner follow-up, and readiness ahead of each audit period.

Extend continuous control validation beyond what our compliance platform covers. Build custom tests with AI coding tools, work with engineering and IT to pull evidence from source systems instead of collecting screenshots, and check that each test proves what the control requires.

Have 3+ years of experience in GRC, internal audit, or security compliance at a SaaS or technology company

Are comfortable working with SOC 2 and ISO 27001 controls, a compliance platform such as Drata, Vanta, and evidence from systems like AWS, GCP, Google Workspace, and Okta

Communicate clearly across both technical and non-technical audiences

Have experience in async or globally distributed teams

Are energized by solving real-world problems for developers

Are comfortable navigating ambiguity and moving quickly

Uncover and understand business and technical requirements.

Work with customers to lead proof-of-value implementations.

Provide regular customer engagements to answer complex questions, unblock adoption, and optimize scaled performance.

translate security concerns into engineering action

can clearly explain complex technical concepts to others

are a fast learner and thrive in ambiguity

Map the security posture of our cloud and production infrastructure and turn it into a documented baseline.

Find the gaps that could lead to customer data loss or infrastructure compromise, agree a prioritized security debt backlog with engineering, and close the highest-risk items first.

Harden identity, secrets and network boundaries, including cloud IAM, environment separation and credential hygiene.

Secure the infrastructure that runs customer-deployed voice AI agents: tenant isolation, secrets for third-party model providers, and media and recording storage.

Build guardrails and secure defaults into our infrastructure as code so the secure path is the easy one for engineers.

Partner with our Compliance team to map infrastructure controls to PCI-DSS and ISO 27001 and automate the technical evidence those audits depend on.

A track record of owning infrastructure security for a production platform.

Fluency with infrastructure as code such as Terraform, and strong scripting and automation skills in a language such as Go or Python.

A pragmatic approach to risk: you can explain why a finding matters to the business and how fast it needs to be fixed.

Experience with real-time media, networking or WebRTC systems.

Experience in AI security, such as securing LLM applications or agent infrastructure.

Incident response or application security experience.

Experience supporting compliance audits such as PCI-DSS, ISO 27001, SOC 2 or HIPAA.
